This needs to be expanded, for which the Federal Government has committed itself with its two coalition parties, and we have worked successfully on it. There were the farmers, the students, the single-earners, the people with atypical employment relationships and the entrepreneurs who have come along. And it is good that family benefits now apply to all families in Austria and not only to those who come from the field of workers. Ladies and gentlemen! However, this success is not only limited to the extension of the reference circle, but has been increased from the former 4 700 S to 6 000 S per month, and the reference period of 24 months has also been extended to 3 years when both parents care for family care and child rearing. - That's good. The harvest for this will be given to the Republic of Austria when the children who are three years old are going to school. Since parents can take care of their children themselves in the early years, less behavioral and less nervous children will come to school, but very well - trained children who have taken care of both parents. The harvest of these family policies will be given to Austrian society in two to three years, and that is also good, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] It is undisputed that the circle of fathers who take advantage of the karenz age has quadrupled – from 1 421 to 5 316 persons – that this is still marginal in relation to the socio-political ideas of this Republic. For example, there have been Year 2004 a monthly multiple-child supplement for child care allowance of € 218 for each additional child, which has been received very positively. The annual health insurance benefits have increased to € 72,673 million for child care professionals, and the budget there will be increased by €66.2 million in the years 2005 to 2008. I am pleased that all these measures are not only in the law, but are also sustainablely secured, and that the Family Compensation Fund will be rebalanced by 2010 so that new and additional family measures can be taken from the Family Compensation Fund from that date on. I should like to remind you that there has been a long discourse on the possibilities of merit in this area. There has also been a marked increase in the short-term limits. As of 2002, there was an increase in the multi-child surcharge, from € 29.1 to € 36.4. That is to say, it was raised by more than a quarter in 2002. In the areas of family hospice and hardness compensation, significant improvements have been made, and I am grateful to the Federal Government for having decided at its meeting on Tuesday this week to move the limit from €500 to €700 and to increase the duration of the care of severely ill children from €6 to a maximum of nine months. I would have liked to have done so as a Social Minister at the time, at that time time the time was not yet ripe for it, but the evaluation has shown that six months are too few, that nine months would be better, and that the limits of commitment, as I have always wanted, should be increased. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] But not only for the younger generation have we done something, but also for the older generation. In addition, we will be voting today as part of the decision-making process of the 65. ASVG-Novelle increase the compensation allowance rate for single persons to € 690, because there is also an urgent need for action in this area. I do not let anyone in the area of social-democratic opposition say that this would not be enough. This issue is particularly important to me, since I am a professional red-cross employee, and I have a lot to do with people affected by it. It is a question of creating a favourable possibility of re-insurance for pension insurance in the form that the Federal Government pays the share of the service provider. The monthly contribution basis is 1 350 €. With this, ladies and gentlemen, we support those families and persons who care for their families at home and sacrifice a lot of time and energy for their families. My experience also shows that, especially in rural areas, many sick people are still being cared for and cared for at home. We also know that the caregivers want to stay at home for as long as possible, in their usual environment. In my home district of Schärding, for example, many people are still being cared for at home. In a community there is no citizen who lives in an old or nursing home. This shows that solidarity and cohesion in these families are still working very well. I am very grateful to the Federal Government for this very important measure on behalf of the caring family.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e12889	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Mrs Silhavy, today, as always, I will represent an independent freedom line, which will of course not always be covered by the line of the Federal Government. That will be the case today, but not with this law. That's the case, you've read it wrong. Just like I'm even sorry that you've jumped in for painters and that's why you don't really see a lot of things right. You're looking for a scandal where no one really is, Professor Grünewald. As President of the Health Committee, which has not yet been presented, I should like to say briefly to colleagues who do not sit in the Health Committee. This is a very pragmatic, objective matter. When Austria came to the EU, we did not have our own dental training, but the dentists were connected to the general medical training as physicians of general medicine and with two years of postgraduate training they were able to practice their dental profession. This was not EU-compliant. There it is stated that there must be an own dental training with at least five years of university studies. There has been a training reform in the course of a university study law. This has been the case since 1997. However, the provisions in the Medical Act were too different from the EU, too different. There was an infringement procedure. As part of this procedure, Austria has agreed to make a more precise distinction between dentists and general medicine doctors in terms of terms and laws. And that's exactly what happened now. There is a law of dentists which distinguishes the professional image of the dentists and is different from that of the doctors. This, of course, results in a medical law novelle. And because there is also an independent Chamber of Dentists in the course of an independent dental training, there is also a need for innovation in the Medical Law – these are the first three templates – and then, of course, other health and social legislation must also make conceptual changes in the course of these innovations. [[Abg. Silhavy: Also on qualitative changes!]] This is the fourth law: the dental reform accompanying law. This is a debate here, and there will be little to be found in what could have been done differently. Until the end – and this is the political – has been negotiated with the medical board. This was involved in all possible changes – and some of them, I agree with Green Forest – and Brettenthaler was satisfied at the end. You have to take note of that. This law did not come about against the standing representation. And as far as constitutional conformity is concerned, there was a meeting between the Federal Minister on 4 May, as well as I would point out. In the case of the relevant constitutional lawyers, which clearly referred to a protocol note from the Council of Ministers in October, the proposed solution is constitutionally permissible. I mean, if something is really factual and pragmatic and it's hard to find a political approach, then you should take note of that, leave the church in the village and not look for scandals where none are.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e12950	Mr President. High house! At the beginning, let me say something about the content of this law very briefly, and not just the law. In addition to this independent professional right for dentists and dentists, we also have numerous improvements for patients, for example the consumer-friendly obligation to inform about the costs incurred and the cost of social security, which is not insignificant especially in the case of the non-insignificant costs of tooth replacement. We have a comprehensive quality assurance in this law and for the first time in a professional law we have already provided for an obligation to ensure access to the disabled for ordinations. That is, I believe, something that is particularly important for disabled people. But let me also briefly report on the reorganisation of the office of the dentists according to the wishes of this professional group. There was a survey, and finally this is a process that lasted a total of 15 years. So this discussion is not new, it has taken about 15 years within the stand representation – it is represented here at the top of the gallery, you can then ask them all afterwards. She also hastened me right after I took up office as Minister of Health and I have also dedicated myself to this subject with the urgency required, because the dentists are a staggering profession. It was also about the survival of the Dentist Chamber, about the financial survival, because it could not even maintain its professional representation. I've been in one, I'd almost say mediation process is trying to accompany that two years. You can compare this with a divorce process. There was a rosary war in stages, with not only the divergent view of dentists and dentists and doctors on the one hand, but also significant differences and differences of opinion within both professional groups on the residence or exit of professional groups on the other. That wasn't an easy process. That's why you're all a little right. Just, Mrs Silhavy, I have to draw your attention to this: As a minister and as a Democrat, I felt obliged to respect democratically developed decisions within a professional group. You are always the lawyers of the subordinate group, but in a democracy you decide 50 plus 1. You may also see this compromise as a divorce comparison, and – this is also important – no one should rejoice both in a compromise and in a divorce comparison, because then one would have been pulled over the table. But it must also be possible for everyone to live and survive with it. I think that with this solution – also with the stay of both groups in the welfare fund – this was also the wish of both groups – not only the survival of both groups, but also the protection of the old age of both groups is guaranteed. I would like to thank all those who were quite clear in this difficult process. I am glad if, with this law-making process, we can resolve a 15-year-old tumultuous dispute, and hope that everyone can live and survive with it.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e13988	Mr President. High house! Austria has had the legal framework for the application of gene analysis and gene therapy to humans for more than ten years under the Genetic Engineering Act, thus having a legislative pioneering role in this area in Europe. However, the particularly high rate of development in all areas of genetic technology means that it is necessary to make continuous normative adjustments to technical progress. The main aim of the present draft is to adapt the area of genetic engineering to the state of science and technology, which is not currently regulated by the EU, with the aim of maintaining and developing an ever-high level of protection and safety. The key points of the novels are the redefinition and differentiation of genetic analyses according to the state of science and technology, a slight deregulation with regard to the administrative procedures for the approval of gene therapy and the authorisation of facilities for certain predictive gene analysis, the taking into account of new technologies and methods, and a more differentiated assessment of the quality, advice and data protection aspects associated with these applications in the light of recent developments in the legal situation. ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e14462	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Both laws, which we decide on this item on the agenda, mainly serve to adapt to EU directives or to prepare for the implementation of EU rules. With the one law we create a advisory board at the Ministry, which is responsible for the training and further training of staff of official control to protect consumer health and thus helps to ensure a high level in this area. The main areas addressed are food safety, feed control, veterinary and animal welfare. Especially in days like this, the field of consumer health must be particularly taken into account. The current situation of these official control bodies is characterised by a co-existence of various training activities both at national and federal level. There are various training regulations in the countries, and between the competent authorities, between the different areas there is only a small coordination and cooperation density, although they are related topics and content. In the sense of consumer health for our citizens, we thus create high-quality education and training, which corresponds to the latest state of science, as well as conditions for uniform learning and examination documents throughout the country and allow for better use of the existing resources of the Federal Government and countries. The second law, the so-called zoonose law, regulates the surveillance of zoonoses and zoonotic agents. For the explanation: Zoonoses are diseases that can be transmitted from animals to humans. The main objective of this law is to improve the cooperation of all the authorities and bodies concerned in areas such as feed, veterinary and food and human resources. Only close and effective cooperation and cooperation between the authorities and between the competent institutions of the Federal Government and countries will enable disease outbreaks to be carefully investigated and the circumstances to be found. This measure is also intended to minimise the risk of human health, and I therefore ask you to support it.

The fact that in our country, especially now in the many panic reports, it must be efficient and fast to clarify zoonose diseases and hazards is a command of the hour. I am pleased that the Members of the other political groups see these points very similar.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e15475	Mrs Frau Bundesminister Rauch-Kallat has now come forward. I give you the order, but you will be interrupted in 3 minutes – in order to deal with the urgent request. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e15496	Mr President. High house! The present draft law essentially serves to implement the revision of European pharmaceutical legislation. On the one hand, the Government template contains the extension of the authorisation, a scheme whereby an approval for the in-transport bridge is in principle permanent after a one-off extension after five years. The arrangements on pharmacovigilance and market surveillance were strengthened, accompanied by an unlimited market authorisation after a one-off re-newal after five years. On the one hand, the periods within which data are to be submitted within the framework of the pharmacovigilance were in principle shortened. Furthermore, in the field of pharmacovigilance, the possibilities of new information technologies should be used to improve the exchange between Member States. The rules on labelling, technical and user information have been harmonised. In the context of labelling, the obligation was also imposed to attach the name of the proprietary medicinal product and the strength to the trade package by using Braille writing, which is particularly important for blind people. A simplified registration procedure has been introduced in connection with so-called herbal medicinal products. This amendment was also taken as an opportunity to review the chapter on advertising restrictions in accordance with the directive. In this context, a ban on the acceptance of natural discounts in respect of proprietary medicinal products listed in the refund code has also been introduced by persons entitled to the prescription or levy. In addition, this Chapter provides for a regulatory authorisation to make further arrangements as regards the scope of grants in connection with the promotion of sales to persons entitled to tender or to issue them with regard to the representation of sales promotion events and scientific congresses. With regard to the problem of the demarcation of different product groups of medicinal products – to be considered, for example, medical devices, biocidal products, cosmetics and primarily food supplements – a standard hierarchy is introduced in the implementation of Article 2 of that Directive, which clearly defines that products that comply with the definition of medicinal products are exclusively subject to the rules on medicinal products, even if the product still has to comply with the definition of other products. Finally, due to current developments, the possibility of making derogations from certain provisions of the Medicines Act for particular situations of crisis has been introduced, provided and as long as this is absolutely necessary to maintain the supply of medicinal products to the population. I would therefore like to thank you for your support for this bill, as far as possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e21033	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I'm still coming. In brief, refer to the amendment of the pharmaceutical, patent and utility law: This draft law provides for the implementation of Community law, so that a pharmaceutical manufacturer may carry out studies and tests during the patent period of a medicinal product that are necessary to develop a generic to the maturity of the authorisation, called Bolar Provision. This will now allow manufacturers to place a generic on the market as soon as the patent expires, as the long preparatory procedures can start before the patent expires. In other countries, such as the USA, Canada, Japan, Australia or Hungary, Bolar Provision is already in force. As we know, generics are considerably cheaper than original medicines, as manufacturers do not have costs for research and development. The share of generic markets on the pharmaceutical market varies in each EU country. The highest share in Germany or Sweden, which is about 40 percent, is 22 percent in the UK and 12% in the Netherlands. In Austria, the share is currently 14.4 percent and in recent years has risen steadily thanks to the pharmaceutical package of our Federal Minister. In 2003, there was a 10.6 percent share and a 12.3 percent share last year. The impact of this law on Austria is, of course, very positive, both in the interest of employment and in the interest of the economic location. It is important that these jobs are located domestically and not abroad. Nothing is more important than creating new knowledge-based jobs in our country in the foreseeable future. The generic market is a future market. So thank you for this law to the ladies and gentlemen of the Federal Ministry and our wife Federal Minister of Smoking.

We have, however, been told by the Constitutional Service that it would be a matter of constitutional concern to take a purely discretionary decision here. We have now adopted the instrument with this postal law and now have the opportunity to use independent experts. It may be prohibited to close a post office. [[Abg. Marizzi: Then!]] We have another instrument in hand. So far, it was not possible for the Minister to keep individual post offices from closing. The ÖIAG operates profit maximizingly. Post AG has to act as profit maximizingly. We are changing the spam regulation today, and I admit that I regret it to a certain extent, because I am firmly convinced that we have effectively complied with the protection objective of the standard, namely to protect natural persons more effectively than companies. In Austria, the law knows the concept of business and the concept of consumerism. However, it was not possible to convince our colleagues in the European Union that we were actually more in line with our definition than with the text of the directive, incorporated into Austrian law 1 : 1. Nevertheless, we did not want it to happen to a procedure and have adapted it accordingly to the text of the directive. But I must say that we must be aware that we do not get rid of the annoying spam emails. Most of them come from abroad. This also means major economic damage, because if we are becoming even more stringent in Austrian law as regards companies – business-to-business business and also direct marketing must be possible in times like these – and make permanent further restrictions, this does not do well in relation to Austrian and European economic growth. However, we have now amended the regulation. The explanations have been discussed and explained with the officials of the European Commission. These interpretation ceilings are likely to be permissible, they are certainly useful. Furthermore, we are changing the universal service, because we already have a market, we no longer need to register the information services and the universal service provider no longer needs to provide this universal service in the future. This is also done in an orderly procedure. The previous committer will be informed. I hope that in the future more universal services will be provided in competition until we no longer need a universal service provider. That would be the optimal state. Thank you.

The result of this study was that we had a very high demand in this area in the years since 2000, because infrastructure development in both the road and rail sectors has stagnated in the years 1985 to 1999 and we have actually fallen in real terms since then, since 2000, also through the opening to the East, of course had enormous need for recovery and even more. This demand for pick-up was also taken very seriously, so that from 2000 to 2004 as a result of this subcommittee, we could see that half more expenditure had been incurred in both the road and rail sectors than in the years before. According to the General Transport Plan, this will continue to increase accordingly – so alone from 2005 to 2009 to more than €13 billion. This is a huge effort, but it pays off. If the opposition – especially the SPÖ and Collaborator Herbs – still believes that only negative things can be recognized, then this simply goes by the reality and also by the figures that have been clearly explained here. I think it would be useful to implement the Court of Auditors' recommendations, which go in the direction of creating a guide that will ensure that in the future costs can be met or reduced, that at the same time the duration of the project can be maintained and that the probability of success of a project can be realistic or enforced. I am particularly pleased that the field of advisory services has become the main subject of today's deliberations and that the Court's recommendations are addressed. I can assure you, after today's motion for a resolution seems to have the majority in place, that we, the Court of Auditors, will in future make sure that the recommendations you make today are actually implemented. An important point of the activity report concerns the Austrian Convention or the positions of the Court of Auditors in the Austrian Convention. Allow me to grab at least two positions. Firstly, I think it is essential that those communities which are certainly well-run, with fewer than 20 000 inhabitants, be subject to the control of the Court of Auditors, not just those with more than 20 000 inhabitants. [[As a result of this, the European Parliament has adopted a number of amendments to the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, including those tabled by the European Parliament and the Council. May. Kogler.]] The current problem is that of 2 359 municipalities 24 are subject to audit by the Court of Auditors and a total of 20 municipalities have a control office. What does that look like in practice? – A municipality has about 23 000 inhabitants and a per capita debt of 129 € – we can test this community. We cannot test a municipality with 16 000 inhabitants and a per capita debt of € 3 870. We can also not check a community with 7,500 inhabitants and a per capita debt of € 3,641. Similarly, we cannot examine communities that, for example, have financial debt of €61 million, although revenue is only half of it. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Parnigoni.]] Without now assuming that the communities are operating badly, I must say: As in other areas, I think it is appropriate and necessary for external financial control to take place. In this context, I would also like to mention that, from the point of view of a macroeconomic perspective or a macroeconomic balance – I think you know Article 13 para. 2, which states that the Federal Government, countries and communities have to work towards a balance – which is a point to be taken into account. The Stability Pact – including the Federal Government, the Länder and the Communities – and in addition the Maastricht criteria do not differ between the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities, but the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities have the overall criteria to meet. Another point concerns undertakings. Businesses will already be able to be subject to participation of 25 percent of the audit by the Court of Auditors – 50 percent now. This is due to the fact that long procedures are often required before the Constitutional Court. For example, the AUA was requested in 2000 or tried to carry out an examination. The examination was challenged, the Constitutional Court was called, and in 2004 it came to the conclusion. That is, we can now, at four years' delay, carry out a review of the AUA. In this context, I would like to mention that this is only a partial “de facto” step – between quotation marks – in the past. It was true that from 1948 to 1977 all undertakings with public participation, including participation of only one percent of the public sector, were subject to a review by the Court of Auditors. On the basis of the deliberations in the Convention, I believe that it is certainly necessary or appropriate, including from the Parliament's point of view, in the context of the right of interconnection, that there is an extension of control here, so that a flight from control can also be avoided. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I would also like to mention that there is also a report on the agenda, according to which follow-up audits have been carried out in connection with INVEKOS or with EAGGF, so that the European Court of Auditors also examines EU direct funding, but on the other hand, the Court of Auditors can carry out the co-financing of a review. This leads to a double-resource commitment. I think it would be useful and necessary for the Court of Auditors to be able to review direct EU funding. In this context, I would also like to mention that the European Court of Auditors is currently carrying out essentially only formal audits. That is, he checks the legality and regularity, but the economy is too short. This also leads, for example, to the following questions: Who makes mistakes? What errors are made? Why are mistakes made? How can one avoid mistakes?, not be answered. I believe that a joint effort should be made in the context of the recasting of the EU budget for 2007 to 2013, so that control is given particular attention and in fact the possibility for national accounting authorities to review EU direct funding. At the same time, benchmark checks should check whether EU directives allow for economic use at all. [[Congratulations on the liberals and the Greens.]] Despite the late hour, I would like to thank you once again for the active support at work. [[Abg. Scheibner: We are still sitting there, Mr President.]] – I know it is, I know it from the past. I would ask you to actively support the work of the Court of Auditors in the past as well as in the past, in particular the efforts to extend financial control, already in your own interest, in the interest of Parliament, that you contribute or help to implement it in reality. [[Abg. May. Kogler: That would be very nice!]] Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e29845	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! By the decision of the Foreign Police Act, Six in 2005 a new situation was created, a situation which is very problematic for agriculture, for the economy and for those concerned, but a general visa requirement for employment as seasonal workers was introduced in Austria. Citizens from the EU candidate countries are affected by Romania, Bulgaria and Croatia, whose citizens are currently free to enter our country. A total of three certificates would have been required for foreigners seeking work. He even had to leave his home country in the short term to apply for a visa. This procedure for the authorities, but also for the workers, is not practical! Especially for harvest workers and those in cultural care, there is often a very short-term need for labour. Prolonged bureaucratic procedures are by no means portable for agricultural holdings. In the worst case, massive harvest losses may occur if workers are not available in good time. For this reason, we are now adopting a legislative amendment that will increase practicality. In the future, the foreign police will check whether there are any foreign-law concerns about an employment permit. If this is not the case, a declaration of safety will be issued for someone who will then receive the AMS employment permit. So this is a legalisation of the situation for seasonal and harvest workers, so far only labour law, and now there is also a legal basis for residence. From the point of view of agriculture, I can only welcome this change. My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen! No additional harvest aid in Austria will be employed by this implementation. The number of seasonal workers is limited by the establishment regulation, which is currently under review. In addition, the AMS was instructed to grant an employment permit only to seasonal eggs who had already worked two seasons in Austria. The fear, Mr Darabos, that 20 000 cheap workers will come to Austria under this title from non-EU Eastern countries, as the trade union fears, is therefore groundless. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This proposal is an important complement to the Foreign Police Act and means greater legal certainty in the labour market. - Thank you.

This is indeed a brilliant unword, a brilliant word creation, and even to where nothing is actually corrected by this law, but rather it becomes a bit more complicated. It is essentially a matter of shooting and explosive equipment which, within the meaning of the Seveso Directive, is particularly dangerous or/and within the meaning of the IPPC Directive. are particularly polluting or large, in which plant law of the industrial regulations is transferred. It's all very complicated. In short, there are a few positive points, because this transfer to the trade regulations will provide for or require a special risk assessment. Emergency plans will be required, public information on the risks of these installations will be improved, and there will also be greater transparency and participation in approval procedures. – So far as the positive part. It is negative from our point of view that the neighbours cannot object in terms of the right of interference. This means that, for example, neighbors of such firearms and explosives can complain about noise and object, but can not object to safety. Therefore, if they consider that there is only an insufficient safety analysis, they cannot assert it and apply it. We consider this to be an essential lack of this law in all positive aspects and will therefore not agree to this law. In this context, I would also like to ask a question. Perhaps there has now been a possibility that we will also receive an answer, because I have already asked that in committee. Countries are required by this law to ensure that the distance between these shooting and explosives and the population is respected with spatial planning innovations. It is therefore necessary, first of all, to adopt these regional regulations in order to exclude any threat to the population. It could not be found in the Committee whether or not the countries have already done so. My question in this regard would be, and perhaps there is still an answer today, whether this has already happened. After all, it is about the health of the population. So it would just be interesting to know that. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] The second point concerns a change in the trade regulations, namely the amendment of the host garden scheme. This is an interesting point when it comes to the health of the population. The background is that the Constitutional Court has removed parts of the host garden scheme, in my view in a meaningful area. It has been found that not the head of state or the head of state – in this case Klasnic, which was the occasion – is responsible for amending the opening hours of host gardens, but that this must be transferred to the community. So far, so good. That is what we think is right, and in that respect the law is in accordance with this Constitutional Court judgment. Our problem is that this change of law is too far away. We believe that the basic problem is that the host gardens are not currently treated as part of the plant and are therefore not individually approved. The operating hours are therefore regulated by law and regulation and the rights of the neighbours are not sufficiently protected from our point of view. Thus, the opening time of a host garden may be extended to 24 hours by regulation. There have been a number of studies carried out by different bodies, especially in Graz, which have found that a noise pollution can actually occur, which is not reasonable for the arrivals. What we want – and in this direction the law should go from our point of view – is that there will be an agreement, an agreement between arrants, arrants and the operators of host gardens and on On the basis of objective decisions, agreement is reached. From our point of view, this law does not at all have the possibility to do so, and that is why we will reject this law.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e31949	We now come to points 32 and 33 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. I call on the rapporteur, Mr Glaser, to present a pressurised correction to point 33. – Mr rapporteur, please make your correction.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e31970	Madam President. High house! I would like to make the following corrections to the Committee's report 1151 of the Supplements: In the title of the text of the law, the second line of words is “with him”. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e31986	I would like to thank the rapporteur for his statement. We are thus opening the debate. The first speaker to speak is Mrs Sburny. Desirable time: 4 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-10-19-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00125_d7e32013	In that case, maybe three will be rich. – The Information Reuse Act is about the implementation of an EU directive, which should have already been implemented since 1. ==References== The aim of this Directive is to lay down minimum rules for the re-use of public sector documents. This means that in principle it is about access to information created, collected and disseminated by the public sector. The disclosure of these documents is also presented in the EU Directive as a fundamental means of extending the right to knowledge. In this sense, this law contains a number of aspects that we have a positive assessment. We also welcome in this matter that the implementation of this law leads to greater transparency. This means that conditions under which information is provided are clarified in advance. We also welcome non-discrimination. Once a document has been made available to third parties, it must also be made available to others, and there is a ban on exclusive agreements, i.e. no contracts for the exclusive use of information. This is the positive part that simply ensures that information freedom can exist in some way. What is not quite clear to me and what could not be answered in the Committee in my view to satisfaction are the following questions: On the one hand: There are rules on public interest. That is to say, both in the case of non-discrimination and in the case of the prohibition of exclusive There are exceptions to public interest rules. This is legally defined but very unclear. From my point of view, there is a certain amount of arbitrariness, which information can or must be passed on and which does not. Apart from that, there is no obligation to provide information anyway. That's fixed anyway. What information should not be passed on yet, because there is supposed to be a public interest, is not clearly defined. Furthermore, this also concerns the ORF, educational institutions, museums, libraries, which are in principle excluded, which is not understandable for the same reason, if there is no obligation to provide information anyway. What are the other exceptions here, in fact, does not open up to me, and I have not received an answer either. Another point is that there is a directive, but ten laws – in typical Austrian ways – that is, a federal law and nine national laws, which is in so far a problem – or at least not quite understandable – as a company, if it has disputes, for example, because it wants data both at federal and national level, then has to strike around with ten procedures, namely civil court for federal data and in nine administrative procedures for the country data. In my opinion, this is not at all acceptable, and a single directive could be adopted with a 15a agreement. Finally, the question of the costs incurred is also unclear. – The cities have also noted this. We will therefore not agree to this IWG in this sense. Thank you.
